preliminary layer sweep with pseudo-CP labels.\n\nRuns 5-metric layer sweep across 25 layers × 4 windows using\nheuristic CP annotations from pseudo_cp_annotations.json.\nBootstrap 1000 for CI on best layers.\n\"\"\"\nimport os, json, time, gc, warnings\nos.environ[\"TOKENIZERS_PARALLELISM\"] = \"false\"\nwarnings.filterwarnings(\"ignore\")\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport torch\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression\nfrom sklearn.metrics import balanced_accuracy_score, precision_score\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\n\nSEED = 42\nN_LAYERS = 25\nHIDDEN_DIM = 4096\nWINDOWS = [1, 3, 5, 15]\nMAX_ITER = 2000\nK_CROSSING = 5\nCROSSING_THRESHOLD = 0.5\nPCA_DIM = 100\nN_BOOTSTRAP = 1000\n\nBASE = Path(\"/root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety\")\nHS_DIR = BASE / \"artifacts/hidden_states_advbench\"\nCP_PATH = BASE / \"artifacts/advbench_preliminary/pseudo_cp_annotations.json\"\nOUTPUT_DIR = BASE / \"artifacts/advbench_preliminary/pseudo_cp_layer_sweep\"\nOUTPUT_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n\n\ndef ts():\n    return time.strftime(\"[%H:%M:%S]\")\n\n\ndef load_traces():\n    with open(CP_PATH) as f:\n        cp_data = json.load(f)\n    annotations = cp_data[\"annotations\"]\n    print(f\"  {len(annotations)} annotated traces in pseudo_cp_annotations.json\", flush=True)\n\n    traces = []\n    for tid, ann in sorted(annotations.items()):\n        pt_path = HS_DIR / f\"{tid}.pt\"\n        if not pt_path.exists():\n            continue\n        d = torch.load(pt_path, map_location=\"cpu\", weights_only=False)\n        hs = d[\"hidden_states\"].float().numpy()\n        cp = ann[\"commitment_point\"]\n        T = min(hs.shape[0], ann[\"total_steps\"])\n        if cp < 1 or cp >= T:\n            continue\n        step_labels = [0 if i < cp else 1 for i in range(T)]\n        traces.append(dict(\n            trace_id=tid,\n            hs=hs[:T],\n            cp=cp,\n            step_labels=step_labels,\n            num_steps=T,\n        ))\n    return traces\n\n\ndef split_60_20_20(traces, seed=SEED):\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    n = len(traces)\n    idx = rng.permutation(n)\n    n_train = int(n * 0.6)\n    n_val = int(n * 0.2)\n    train = [traces[i] for i in idx[:n_train]]\n    val = [traces[i] for i in idx[n_train:n_train + n_val]]\n    test = [traces[i] for i in idx[n_train + n_val:]]\n    return train, val, test\n\n\ndef build_windowed_features(traces, layer_idx, window):\n    X_list = []\n    n_per_trace = []\n    for t in traces:\n        hs_layer = t[\"hs\"][:, layer_idx, :]\n        T = hs_layer.shape[0]\n        padded = np.concatenate([np.repeat(hs_layer[:1], window - 1, axis=0), hs_layer], axis=0)\n        windows = np.empty((T, window * HIDDEN_DIM), dtype=hs_layer.dtype)\n        for w_i in range(window):\n            windows[:, w_i * HIDDEN_DIM:(w_i + 1) * HIDDEN_DIM] = padded[w_i:w_i + T]\n        X_list.append(windows)\n        n_per_trace.append(T)\n    return np.vstack(X_list), n_per_trace\n\n\ndef get_labels(traces):\n    y = []\n    for t in traces:\n        y.extend(t[\"step_labels\"])\n    return np.array(y, dtype=np.int32)\n\n\ndef first_crossing(probs, threshold=CROSSING_THRESHOLD, k=K_CROSSING):\n    for i in range(len(probs) - k + 1):\n        if all(p > threshold for p in probs[i:i + k]):\n            return i\n    return None\n\n\ndef evaluate_layer_window(X_train, y_train, X_test, y_test, test_traces, n_test):\n    if len(np.unique(y_train)) < 2:\n        return None\n\n    scaler = StandardScaler()\n    X_tr = scaler.fit_transform(X_train)\n    X_te = scaler.transform(X_test)\n\n    clf = LogisticRegression(\n        solver=\"lbfgs\", C=1.0, class_weight=\"balanced\",\n        max_iter=MAX_ITER, random_state=SEED,\n    )\n    clf.fit(X_tr, y_train)\n\n    y_pred = clf.predict(X_te)\n    y_proba = clf.predict_proba(X_te)[:, 1]\n\n    ba = balanced_accuracy_score(y_test, y_pred)\n    prec = precision_score(y_test, y_pred, zero_division=0)\n\n    fprs_list = []\n    n_crossings = 0\n    offset = 0\n    for i, t in enumerate(test_traces):\n        T = n_test[i]\n        cp = t[\"cp\"]\n        preds_t = y_pred[offset:offset + T]\n        probs_t = y_proba[offset:offset + T]\n\n        if cp > 0:\n            pre_cp_preds = preds_t[:min(T, cp)]\n            if len(pre_cp_preds) > 0:\n                fprs_list.append(float(pre_cp_preds.mean()))\n\n        fc = first_crossing(probs_t.tolist())\n        if fc is not None and fc < cp:\n            n_crossings += 1\n        offset += T\n\n    pre_cp_fpr = float(np.mean(fprs_list)) if fprs_list else 0.0\n    crossing_rate = n_crossings / len(test_traces) if len(test_traces) > 0 else 0.0\n\n    return {\n        \"bal_acc\": round(ba, 4),\n        \"precision\": round(prec, 4),\n        \"fpr\": round(pre_cp_fpr, 4),\n        \"crossing_rate\": round(crossing_rate, 4),\n        \"composite\": round(ba * crossing_rate, 4),\n    }\n\n\ndef bootstrap_ci(traces_pool, layer_idx, window, n_boot=N_BOOTSTRAP):\n    \"\"\"Bootstrap CI by resampling traces. PCA fitted once on all data.\"\"\"\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(SEED + 999)\n    n = len(traces_pool)\n    metrics_boot = {m: [] for m in [\"bal_acc\", \"precision\", \"fpr\", \"crossing_rate\", \"composite\"]}\n\n    X_all, n_per_trace = build_windowed_features(traces_pool, layer_idx, window)\n    pca_dim = min(PCA_DIM, X_all.shape[1], X_all.shape[0])\n    pca = PCA(n_components=pca_dim, random_state=SEED)\n    X_all_pca = pca.fit_transform(X_all)\n    del X_all\n\n    per_trace_X = []\n    offset = 0\n    for nt in n_per_trace:\n        per_trace_X.append(X_all_pca[offset:offset + nt])\n        offset += nt\n    del X_all_pca\n\n    for b in range(n_boot):\n        idx = rng.choice(n, size=n, replace=True)\n        perm = rng.permutation(len(idx))\n        n_train = int(len(idx) * 0.6)\n        n_val = int(len(idx) * 0.2)\n        tr_idx = idx[perm[:n_train + n_val]]\n        te_idx = idx[perm[n_train + n_val:]]\n\n        X_train_pca = np.vstack([per_trace_X[i] for i in tr_idx])\n        X_test_pca = np.vstack([per_trace_X[i] for i in te_idx])\n        y_train = np.concatenate([np.array(traces_pool[i][\"step_labels\"]) for i in tr_idx])\n        y_test_arr = np.concatenate([np.array(traces_pool[i][\"step_labels\"]) for i in te_idx])\n        test_traces = [traces_pool[i] for i in te_idx]\n        n_test = [per_trace_X[i].shape[0] for i in te_idx]\n\n        result = evaluate_layer_window(X_train_pca, y_train, X_test_pca, y_test_arr, test_traces, n_test)\n        if result is None:\n            continue\n        for m in metrics_boot:\n            metrics_boot[m].append(result[m])\n\n        if (b + 1) % 200 == 0:\n            print(f\" [{b+1}/{n_boot}]\", end=\"\", flush=True)\n\n    ci = {}\n    for m, vals in metrics_boot.items():\n        if len(vals) < 10:\n            ci[m] = {\"mean\": None, \"ci_lo\": None, \"ci_hi\": None, \"n_valid\": len(vals)}\n            continue\n        ci[m] = {\n            \"mean\": round(float(np.mean(vals)), 4),\n            \"ci_lo\": round(float(np.percentile(vals, 2.5)), 4),\n            \"ci_hi\": round(float(np.percentile(vals, 97.5)), 4),\n            \"n_valid\": len(vals),\n        }\n    return ci\n\n\ndef main():\n    t_start = time.time()\n\n    # --- Dry run flag ---\n    dry_run = os.environ.get(\"DRY_RUN\", \"0\") == \"1\"\n    if dry_run:\n        print(f\"{ts()} *** DRY RUN: 1 layer, 1 window ***\", flush=True)\n        layers_to_sweep = [12]\n        windows_to_sweep = [3]\n    else:\n        layers_to_sweep = list(range(N_LAYERS))\n        windows_to_sweep = WINDOWS\n\n    print(f\"{ts()} Loading traces...\", flush=True)\n    traces = load_traces()\n    print(f\"  Loaded {len(traces)} traces with pseudo-CP\", flush=True)\n\n    train, val, test = split_60_20_20(traces)\n    train_val = train + val\n    print(f\"  Split: train={len(train)}, val={len(val)}, test={len(test)}\", flush=True)\n\n    # --- Main sweep ---\n    all_results = {}\n    metric_names = [\"bal_acc\", \"precision\", \"fpr\", \"crossing_rate\", \"composite\"]\n    n_combos = len(layers_to_sweep) * len(windows_to_sweep)\n    combo_idx = 0\n\n    for layer_idx in layers_to_sweep:\n        all_results[layer_idx] = {}\n\n        for window in windows_to_sweep:\n            combo_idx += 1\n            t_combo = time.time()\n\n            X_train_raw, _ = build_windowed_features(train_val, layer_idx, window)\n            X_test_raw, n_test = build_windowed_features(test, layer_idx, window)\n            y_train = get_labels(train_val)\n            y_test = get_labels(test)\n\n            pca_dim = min(PCA_DIM, X_train_raw.shape[1], X_train_raw.shape[0])\n            pca = PCA(n_components=pca_dim, random_state=SEED)\n            X_train_pca = pca.fit_transform(X_train_raw)\n            X_test_pca = pca.transform(X_test_raw)\n            ev = pca.explained_variance_ratio_.sum()\n\n            del X_train_raw, X_test_raw\n\n            result = evaluate_layer_window(X_train_pca, y_train, X_test_pca, y_test, test, n_test)\n            all_results[layer_idx][window] = result\n\n            elapsed = time.time() - t_combo\n            if result:\n                print(f\"  [{combo_idx}/{n_combos}] L{layer_idx:2d} W{window:2d}: \"\n                      f\"ba={result['bal_acc']:.3f} prec={result['precision']:.3f} \"\n                      f\"fpr={result['fpr']:.3f} cr={result['crossing_rate']:.3f} \"\n                      f\"comp={result['composite']:.3f} (PCA {ev:.2f}, {elapsed:.1f}s)\",\n                      flush=True)\n            else:\n                print(f\"  [{combo_idx}/{n_combos}] L{layer_idx:2d} W{window:2d}: SKIPPED\", flush=True)\n\n    # Free HS memory\n    for t in traces:\n        del t[\"hs\"]\n    gc.collect()\n\n    # --- Find best layers ---\n    print(f\"\\n{ts()} Finding best layers per metric...\", flush=True)\n    best_layers = {}\n    for m in metric_names:\n        best_val = -1e9 if m != \"fpr\" else 1e9\n        best_key = None\n        for layer_idx in layers_to_sweep:\n            for window in windows_to_sweep:\n                r = all_results[layer_idx].get(window)\n                if r is None:\n                    continue\n                metric_val = r[m]\n                if m == \"fpr\":\n                    if metric_val < best_val:\n                        best_val = metric_val\n                        best_key = (layer_idx, window)\n                else:\n                    if metric_val > best_val:\n                        best_val = metric_val\n                        best_key = (layer_idx, window)\n        best_layers[m] = {\"layer\": best_key[0], \"window\": best_key[1], \"value\": best_val}\n\n    # Per-window best\n    best_per_window = {}\n    for m in metric_names:\n        best_per_window[m] = {}\n        for window in windows_to_sweep:\n            best_val = -1e9 if m != \"fpr\" else 1e9\n            best_l = None\n            for layer_idx in layers_to_sweep:\n                r = all_results[layer_idx].get(window)\n                if r is None:\n                    continue\n                metric_val = r[m]\n                if m == \"fpr\":\n                    if metric_val < best_val:\n                        best_val = metric_val\n                        best_l = layer_idx\n                else:\n                    if metric_val > best_val:\n                        best_val = metric_val\n                        best_l = layer_idx\n            best_per_window[m][f\"W{window}\"] = {\n                \"layer\": f\"L{best_l}\",\n                \"value\": round(best_val, 4),\n            }\n\n    # --- Print summary table ---\n    print(f\"\\n{'='*70}\", flush=True)\n    print(f\"Best layers per metric × window (AdvBench, pseudo-CP):\", flush=True)\n    print(f\"{'Metric':16s}\", end=\"\")\n    for w in windows_to_sweep:\n        print(f\" {'W='+str(w):>8s}\", end=\"\")\n    print()\n    print(\"-\" * (16 + 9 * len(windows_to_sweep)))\n    for m in metric_names:\n        print(f\"{m:16s}\", end=\"\")\n        for w in windows_to_sweep:\n            info = best_per_window[m][f\"W{w}\"]\n            print(f\" {info['layer']:>8s}\", end=\"\")\n        print()\n    print(f\"{'='*70}\", flush=True)\n\n    # --- Bootstrap CI for best layers ---\n    if not dry_run:\n        print(f\"\\n{ts()} Bootstrap {N_BOOTSTRAP}x for best layers...\", flush=True)\n        # Reload traces with HS for bootstrap\n        traces_reload = load_traces()\n        bootstrap_results = {}\n        for m in metric_names:\n            bl = best_layers[m]\n            key = f\"L{bl['layer']}_W{bl['window']}\"\n            if key in bootstrap_results:\n                bootstrap_results[f\"{m}_best\"] = bootstrap_results[key]\n                continue\n            print(f\"  {m}: L{bl['layer']} W{bl['window']}...\", end=\"\", flush=True)\n            t_boot = time.time()\n            ci = bootstrap_ci(traces_reload, bl[\"layer\"], bl[\"window\"])\n            bootstrap_results[key] = ci\n            bootstrap_results[f\"{m}_best\"] = ci\n            print(f\" done ({time.time()-t_boot:.0f}s)\", flush=True)\n            for mm, vals in ci.items():\n                if vals[\"mean\"] is not None:\n                    print(f\"    {mm}: {vals['mean']:.3f} [{vals['ci_lo']:.3f}, {vals['ci_hi']:.3f}]\",\n                          flush=True)\n        del traces_reload\n    else:\n        bootstrap_results = {}\n\n    # --- Full table ---\n    full_table = {}\n    for m in metric_names:\n        full_table[m] = {}\n        for layer_idx in layers_to_sweep:\n            full_table[m][f\"L{layer_idx}\"] = {}\n            for window in windows_to_sweep:\n                r = all_results[layer_idx].get(window)\n                full_table[m][f\"L{layer_idx}\"][f\"W{window}\"] = r[m] if r else None\n\n    # --- Save ---\n    elapsed = time.time() - t_start\n    output = {\n        \"experiment\": \"advbench_preliminary_pseudo_cp_sweep\",\n        \"model\": \"DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-8B\",\n        \"dataset\": \"AdvBench (B-class, pseudo-CP)\",\n        \"method\": \"heuristic_max_cosine_dist\",\n        \"n_traces\": len(traces),\n        \"split\": {\"train\": len(train), \"val\": len(val), \"test\": len(test)},\n        \"seed\": SEED,\n        \"layers\": len(layers_to_sweep),\n        \"layer_range\": f\"L{layers_to_sweep[0]}-L{layers_to_sweep[-1]}\",\n        \"windows\": windows_to_sweep,\n        \"metrics\": metric_names,\n        \"pca_dim\": PCA_DIM,\n        \"max_iter\": MAX_ITER,\n        \"crossing_k\": K_CROSSING,\n        \"crossing_threshold\": CROSSING_THRESHOLD,\n        \"n_bootstrap\": N_BOOTSTRAP if not dry_run else 0,\n        \"runtime_seconds\": round(elapsed, 1),\n        \"best_layers_overall\": {m: best_layers[m] for m in metric_names},\n        \"best_layers_per_window\": best_per_window,\n        \"full_table\": full_table,\n        \"bootstrap_ci\": bootstrap_results,\n    }\n\n    out_path = OUTPUT_DIR / \"sweep_results.json\"\n    with open(out_path, \"w\") as f:\n        json.dump(output, f, indent=2)\n    print(f\"\\n{ts()} Saved to {out_path}\", flush=True)\n\n    # --- Human-readable report ---\n    lines = [\n        f\"AdvBench Preliminary Layer Sweep (pseudo-CP)\",\n        \"=\" * 55,\n        f\"Traces: {len(traces)} (B-class compliance, heuristic CP)\",\n        f\"Layers: {len(layers_to_sweep)}, Windows: {windows_to_sweep}\",\n        f\"Split: {len(train)}/{len(val)}/{len(test)}, Seed: {SEED}\",\n        f\"PCA: {PCA_DIM}d, LR: C=1.0 balanced max_iter={MAX_ITER}\",\n        f\"Bootstrap: {N_BOOTSTRAP if not dry_run else 0}\",\n        \"\",\n        f\"{'Metric':16s}\",\n    ]\n    header = f\"{'Metric':16s}\"\n    for w in windows_to_sweep:\n        header += f\" {'W='+str(w):>8s}\"\n    lines.append(header)\n    lines.append(\"-\" * (16 + 9 * len(windows_to_sweep)))\n    for m in metric_names:\n        row = f\"{m:16s}\"\n        for w in windows_to_sweep:\n            info = best_per_window[m][f\"W{w}\"]\n            row += f\" {info['layer']:>8s}\"\n        lines.append(row)\n\n    lines.append(\"\")\n    lines.append(\"Best layers with values:\")\n    for m in metric_names:\n        bl = best_layers[m]\n        lines.append(f\"  {m}: L{bl['layer']} W{bl['window']} = {bl['value']:.4f}\")\n\n    if bootstrap_results:\n        lines.append(\"\")\n        lines.append(\"Bootstrap 95% CI (best layer per metric):\")\n        for m in metric_names:\n            ci = bootstrap_results.get(f\"{m}_best\", {}).get(m, {})\n            if ci and ci.get(\"mean\") is not None:\n                lines.append(f\"  {m}: {ci['mean']:.3f} [{ci['ci_lo']:.3f}, {ci['ci_hi']:.3f}]\")\n\n    lines.append(f\"\\nTotal time: {elapsed:.0f}s\")\n    report = \"\\n\".join(lines)\n\n    with open(OUTPUT_DIR / \"report.txt\", \"w\") as f:\n        f.write(report)\n    print(f\"\\n{report}\", flush=True)\n    print(f\"\\n{ts()} DONE.\", flush=True)\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n","numLines":461,"startLine":1,"totalLines":461}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0174hg226ceaGcBHTbDnFGBC","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Extract hidden states from DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Llama-8B for each sentence in traces.\n2\t\n3\tInput: List of trace dicts (from data_loader.prepare_dataset)\n4\tOutput: Per-trace .pt files with tensor [num_sentences, 13, 4096] + metadata\n5\t\"\"\"\n6\t\n7\timport os\n8\tfrom pathlib import Path\n9\tfrom typing import List, Dict\n10\t\n11\timport torch\n12\tfrom transformers import AutoTokenizer, AutoModelForCausalLM\n13\tfrom tqdm import tqdm\n14\t\n15\tfrom .config import (\n16\t    MODEL_PATH, LAYERS, HIDDEN_DIM, NUM_LAYERS,\n17\t    HIDDEN_STATES_DIR, EXTRACT_BATCH_SIZE, DEVICE\n18\t)\n19\t\n20\t\n21\tdef load_model(model_path: str = MODEL_PATH, device: str = DEVICE):\n22\t    \"\"\"Load model and tokenizer.\"\"\"\n23\t    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(model_path, trust_remote_code=True)\n24\t    if tokenizer.pad_token is None:\n25\t        tokenizer.pad_token = tokenizer.eos_token\n26\t\n27\t    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(\n28\t        model_path,\n29\t        torch_dtype=torch.float16,\n30\t        device_map=device,\n31\t        trust_remote_code=True,\n32\t    )\n33\t    model.eval()\n34\t    return model, tokenizer\n35\t\n36\t\n37\tdef find_sentence_boundaries(tokenizer, sentences: List[str]) -> List[int]:\n38\t    \"\"\"Find each sentence's last token position via char-to-token offset mapping.\n39\t\n40\t    Tokenizes the full joined text once, then maps each sentence's char span\n41\t    to token positions. This avoids BPE context mismatch from per-sentence tokenization.\n42\t    \"\"\"\n43\t    full_text = \" \".join(sentences)\n44\t    encoding = tokenizer(\n45\t        full_text,\n46\t        return_offsets_mapping=True,\n47\t        return_tensors=\"pt\",\n48\t        truncation=True,\n49\t        max_length=8192,\n50\t    )\n51\t    offsets = encoding[\"offset_mapping\"][0]  # (num_tokens, 2)\n52\t\n53\t    boundaries = []\n54\t    char_pos = 0\n55\t    for sent in sentences:\n56\t        char_end = char_pos + len(sent)\n57\t        last_token_idx = 0\n58\t        for tok_idx, (start, end) in enumerate(offsets):\n59\t            if start < char_end:\n60\t                last_token_idx = tok_idx\n61\t        boundaries.append(last_token_idx)\n62\t        char_pos = char_end + 1  # +1 for the joining space\n63\t\n64\t    return boundaries\n65\t\n66\t\n67\tdef extract_trace_hidden_states(\n68\t    model, tokenizer, sentences: List[str], device: str = DEVICE\n69\t) -> torch.Tensor:\n70\t    \"\"\"Extract hidden states for a single trace.\n71\t\n72\t    Concatenates all sentences, does one forward pass, extracts hidden states\n73\t    at sentence boundary positions for specified layers.\n74\t\n75\t    Returns: tensor of shape [num_sentences, num_layers, hidden_dim]\n76\t    \"\"\"\n77\t    # Find sentence boundaries\n78\t    boundary_positions = find_sentence_boundaries(tokenizer, sentences)\n79\t\n80\t    # Tokenize full text\n81\t    full_text = \" \".join(sentences)\n82\t    inputs = tokenizer(\n83\t        full_text,\n84\t        return_tensors=\"pt\",\n85\t        truncation=True,\n86\t        max_length=8192,\n87\t    ).to(device)\n88\t\n89\t    num_tokens = inputs[\"input_ids\"].shape[1]\n90\t\n91\t    # Clamp boundary positions to valid range\n92\t    boundary_positions = [min(p, num_tokens - 1) for p in boundary_positions]\n93\t\n94\t    with torch.no_grad():\n95\t        outputs = model(**inputs, output_hidden_states=True)\n96\t\n97\t    # outputs.hidden_states is tuple of (num_layers+1) tensors, each [1, seq_len, hidden_dim]\n98\t    # Index 0 is embedding layer, so layer i is at index i+1... but model layers are 0-indexed\n99\t    # For LlamaForCausalLM, hidden_states[0] = embeddings, hidden_states[i] = layer i output\n100\t    hidden_states = outputs.hidden_states\n101\t\n102\t    # Extract specified layers at boundary positions\n103\t    num_sentences = len(sentences)\n104\t    result = torch.zeros(num_sentences, NUM_LAYERS, HIDDEN_DIM, dtype=torch.float16)\n105\t\n106\t    for sent_idx, pos in enumerate(boundary_positions):\n107\t        for layer_idx, layer_num in enumerate(LAYERS):\n108\t            # hidden_states[layer_num + 1] because index 0 is the embedding\n109\t            hs = hidden_states[layer_num + 1][0, pos, :]\n110\t            result[sent_idx, layer_idx, :] = hs.cpu()\n111\t\n112\t    return result\n113\t\n114\t\n115\tdef extract_all_hidden_states(\n116\t    traces: List[Dict],\n117\t    model_path: str = MODEL_PATH,\n118\t    output_dir: str = None,\n119\t    device: str = DEVICE,\n120\t    batch_size: int = EXTRACT_BATCH_SIZE,\n121\t):\n122\t    \"\"\"Extract hidden states for all traces with checkpoint resumption.\n123\t\n124\t    Saves per-trace .pt files containing:\n125\t    - hidden_states: tensor [num_sentences, 13, 4096]\n126\t    - trace_id: str\n127\t    - num_sentences: int\n128\t    - commitment_point: int or None\n129\t    \"\"\"\n130\t    if output_dir is None:\n131\t        output_dir = str(HIDDEN_STATES_DIR)\n132\t\n133\t    os.makedirs(output_dir, exist_ok=True)\n134\t\n135\t    # Check which traces already have extracted states\n136\t    existing = set()\n137\t    for f in Path(output_dir).glob(\"*.pt\"):\n138\t        existing.add(f.stem)\n139\t\n140\t    remaining = [t for t in traces if t[\"trace_id\"] not in existing]\n141\t\n142\t    if not remaining:\n143\t        print(f\"All {len(traces)} traces already extracted. Skipping.\")\n144\t        return\n145\t\n146\t    print(f\"Extracting {len(remaining)}/{len(traces)} traces (skipping {len(existing)} existing)\")\n147\t\n148\t    model, tokenizer = load_model(model_path, device)\n149\t\n150\t    for trace in tqdm(remaining, desc=\"Extracting hidden states\"):\n151\t        trace_id = trace[\"trace_id\"]\n152\t        sentences = trace[\"sentences\"]\n153\t\n154\t        hidden_states = extract_trace_hidden_states(model, tokenizer, sentences, device)\n155\t\n156\t        save_dict = {\n157\t            \"hidden_states\": hidden_states,\n158\t            \"trace_id\": trace_id,\n159\t            \"num_sentences\": len(sentences),\n160\t            \"commitment_point\": trace.get(\"commitment_point\"),\n161\t            \"step_labels\": trace.get(\"step_labels\"),\n162\t        }\n163\t\n164\t        save_path = Path(output_dir) / f\"{trace_id}.pt\"\n165\t        torch.save(save_dict, save_path)\n166\t\n167\t    print(f\"Extraction complete. Files saved to {output_dir}\")\n168\t\n169\t\n170\tdef load_hidden_states(trace_id: str, hidden_states_dir: str = None) -> Dict:\n171\t    \"\"\"Load extracted hidden states for a single trace.\"\"\"\n172\t    if hidden_states_dir is None:\n173\t        hidden_states_dir = str(HIDDEN_STATES_DIR)\n174\t    path = Path(hidden_states_dir) / f\"{trace_id}.pt\"\n175\t    return torch.load(path, map_location=\"cpu\", weights_only=False)\n176\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e51f3ca8-7ac5-4490-8b2a-be7d501298c2","uuid":"38f90ee1-a23e-43fe-999f-c3b2967b9d49","timestamp":"2026-05-23T11:30:27.521Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/src/extract_hidden_states.py","content":"\"\"\"Extract hidden states from DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Llama-8B for each sentence in traces.\n\nInput: List of trace dicts (from data_loader.prepare_dataset)\nOutput: Per-trace .pt files with tensor [num_sentences, 13, 4096] + metadata\n\"\"\"\n\nimport os\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import List, Dict\n\nimport torch\nfrom transformers import AutoTokenizer, AutoModelForCausalLM\nfrom tqdm import tqdm\n\nfrom .config import (\n    MODEL_PATH, LAYERS, HIDDEN_DIM, NUM_LAYERS,\n    HIDDEN_STATES_DIR, EXTRACT_BATCH_SIZE, DEVICE\n)\n\n\ndef load_model(model_path: str = MODEL_PATH, device: str = DEVICE):\n    \"\"\"Load model and tokenizer.\"\"\"\n    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(model_path, trust_remote_code=True)\n    if tokenizer.pad_token is None:\n        tokenizer.pad_token = tokenizer.eos_token\n\n    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(\n        model_path,\n        torch_dtype=torch.float16,\n        device_map=device,\n        trust_remote_code=True,\n    )\n    model.eval()\n    return model, tokenizer\n\n\ndef find_sentence_boundaries(tokenizer, sentences: List[str]) -> List[int]:\n    \"\"\"Find each sentence's last token position via char-to-token offset mapping.\n\n    Tokenizes the full joined text once, then maps each sentence's char span\n    to token positions. This avoids BPE context mismatch from per-sentence tokenization.\n    \"\"\"\n    full_text = \" \".join(sentences)\n    encoding = tokenizer(\n        full_text,\n        return_offsets_mapping=True,\n        return_tensors=\"pt\",\n        truncation=True,\n        max_length=8192,\n    )\n    offsets = encoding[\"offset_mapping\"][0]  # (num_tokens, 2)\n\n    boundaries = []\n    char_pos = 0\n    for sent in sentences:\n        char_end = char_pos + len(sent)\n        last_token_idx = 0\n        for tok_idx, (start, end) in enumerate(offsets):\n            if start < char_end:\n                last_token_idx = tok_idx\n        boundaries.append(last_token_idx)\n        char_pos = char_end + 1  # +1 for the joining space\n\n    return boundaries\n\n\ndef extract_trace_hidden_states(\n    model, tokenizer, sentences: List[str], device: str = DEVICE\n) -> torch.Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Extract hidden states for a single trace.\n\n    Concatenates all sentences, does one forward pass, extracts hidden states\n    at sentence boundary positions for specified layers.\n\n    Returns: tensor of shape [num_sentences, num_layers, hidden_dim]\n    \"\"\"\n    # Find sentence boundaries\n    boundary_positions = find_sentence_boundaries(tokenizer, sentences)\n\n    # Tokenize full text\n    full_text = \" \".join(sentences)\n    inputs = tokenizer(\n        full_text,\n        return_tensors=\"pt\",\n        truncation=True,\n        max_length=8192,\n    ).to(device)\n\n    num_tokens = inputs[\"input_ids\"].shape[1]\n\n    # Clamp boundary positions to valid range\n    boundary_positions = [min(p, num_tokens - 1) for p in boundary_positions]\n\n    with torch.no_grad():\n        outputs = model(**inputs, output_hidden_states=True)\n\n    # outputs.hidden_states is tuple of (num_layers+1) tensors, each [1, seq_len, hidden_dim]\n    # Index 0 is embedding layer, so layer i is at index i+1... but model layers are 0-indexed\n    # For LlamaForCausalLM, hidden_states[0] = embeddings, hidden_states[i] = layer i output\n    hidden_states = outputs.hidden_states\n\n    # Extract specified layers at boundary positions\n    num_sentences = len(sentences)\n    result = torch.zeros(num_sentences, NUM_LAYERS, HIDDEN_DIM, dtype=torch.float16)\n\n    for sent_idx, pos in enumerate(boundary_positions):\n        for layer_idx, layer_num in enumerate(LAYERS):\n            # hidden_states[layer_num + 1] because index 0 is the embedding\n            hs = hidden_states[layer_num + 1][0, pos, :]\n            result[sent_idx, layer_idx, :] = hs.cpu()\n\n    return result\n\n\ndef extract_all_hidden_states(\n    traces: List[Dict],\n    model_path: str = MODEL_PATH,\n    output_dir: str = None,\n    device: str = DEVICE,\n    batch_size: int = EXTRACT_BATCH_SIZE,\n):\n    \"\"\"Extract hidden states for all traces with checkpoint resumption.\n\n    Saves per-trace .pt files containing:\n    - hidden_states: tensor [num_sentences, 13, 4096]\n    - trace_id: str\n    - num_sentences: int\n    - commitment_point: int or None\n    \"\"\"\n    if output_dir is None:\n        output_dir = str(HIDDEN_STATES_DIR)\n\n    os.makedirs(output_dir, exist_ok=True)\n\n    # Check which traces already have extracted states\n    existing = set()\n    for f in Path(output_dir).glob(\"*.pt\"):\n        existing.add(f.stem)\n\n    remaining = [t for t in traces if t[\"trace_id\"] not in existing]\n\n    if not remaining:\n        print(f\"All {len(traces)} traces already extracted. Skipping.\")\n        return\n\n    print(f\"Extracting {len(remaining)}/{len(traces)} traces (skipping {len(existing)} existing)\")\n\n    model, tokenizer = load_model(model_path, device)\n\n    for trace in tqdm(remaining, desc=\"Extracting hidden states\"):\n        trace_id = trace[\"trace_id\"]\n        sentences = trace[\"sentences\"]\n\n        hidden_states = extract_trace_hidden_states(model, tokenizer, sentences, device)\n\n        save_dict = {\n            \"hidden_states\": hidden_states,\n            \"trace_id\": trace_id,\n            \"num_sentences\": len(sentences),\n            \"commitment_point\": trace.get(\"commitment_point\"),\n            \"step_labels\": trace.get(\"step_labels\"),\n        }\n\n        save_path = Path(output_dir) / f\"{trace_id}.pt\"\n        torch.save(save_dict, save_path)\n\n    print(f\"Extraction complete. Files saved to {output_dir}\")\n\n\ndef load_hidden_states(trace_id: str, hidden_states_dir: str = None) -> Dict:\n    \"\"\"Load extracted hidden states for a single trace.\"\"\"\n    if hidden_states_dir is None:\n        hidden_states_dir = str(HIDDEN_STATES_DIR)\n    path = Path(hidden_states_dir) / f\"{trace_id}.pt\"\n    return torch.load(path, map_location=\"cpu\", weights_only=False)\n","numLines":176,"startLine":1,"totalLines":176}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01UZSkJSFdiQfAAKASKvpoq1","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Load HarmThoughts dataset and extract commitment points.\n2\t\n3\tInput: HuggingFace dataset \"ishitakakkar-10/HarmThoughts\"\n4\tOutput: List of trace dicts with sentences, labels, commitment_point, split assignments\n5\t\"\"\"\n6\t\n7\tfrom collections import defaultdict\n8\tfrom typing import List, Optional, Dict, Any\n9\timport numpy as np\n10\tfrom datasets import load_dataset\n11\t\n12\tfrom .config import (\n13\t    HF_DATASET, TARGET_MODEL, SAFETY_LABELS,\n14\t    TRAIN_RATIO, VAL_RATIO, SEED\n15\t)\n16\t\n17\t\n18\tdef load_harmthoughts(model_name: str = TARGET_MODEL) -> Dict[str, List[Dict]]:\n19\t    \"\"\"Load HarmThoughts from HF and group by trace_id.\"\"\"\n20\t    ds = load_dataset(HF_DATASET, split=\"train\")\n21\t\n22\t    traces = defaultdict(list)\n23\t    for row in ds:\n24\t        if row[\"model_name\"] != model_name:\n25\t            continue\n26\t        # trace_id is the part before the last dash in sentence_id\n27\t        sid = row[\"sentence_id\"]\n28\t        parts = sid.rsplit(\"-\", 1)\n29\t        trace_id = parts[0]\n30\t        step_idx = int(parts[1]) - 1  # convert to 0-indexed\n31\t\n32\t        traces[trace_id].append({\n33\t            \"sentence_id\": sid,\n34\t            \"step_idx\": step_idx,\n35\t            \"sentence\": row[\"sentence\"],\n36\t            \"label\": row[\"llm_annotation\"],\n37\t            \"query\": row[\"query\"],\n38\t            \"final_judgment\": row[\"final_judgment\"],\n39\t        })\n40\t\n41\t    # Sort each trace by step_idx\n42\t    for tid in traces:\n43\t        traces[tid].sort(key=lambda x: x[\"step_idx\"])\n44\t\n45\t    return dict(traces)\n46\t\n47\t\n48\tdef extract_commitment_point(labels: List[str]) -> Optional[int]:\n49\t    \"\"\"Find the commitment point in a sequence of labels.\n50\t\n51\t    Returns the index after the last safety label, or None if:\n52\t    - No safety labels exist in the trace\n53\t    - The last safety label is at the final position (no post-commitment segment)\n54\t    \"\"\"\n55\t    last_safety_idx = None\n56\t    for i, label in enumerate(labels):\n57\t        if label in SAFETY_LABELS:\n58\t            last_safety_idx = i\n59\t\n60\t    if last_safety_idx is None:\n61\t        return None\n62\t    if last_safety_idx == len(labels) - 1:\n63\t        return None\n64\t\n65\t    return last_safety_idx + 1\n66\t\n67\t\n68\tdef create_step_labels(commitment_point: int, num_steps: int) -> List[int]:\n69\t    \"\"\"Generate binary labels: 0 = pre-commitment, 1 = post-commitment.\"\"\"\n70\t    return [0 if i < commitment_point else 1 for i in range(num_steps)]\n71\t\n72\t\n73\tdef prepare_dataset(model_name: str = TARGET_MODEL) -> Dict[str, List[Dict[str, Any]]]:\n74\t    \"\"\"Load data, compute commitment points, categorize traces.\n75\t\n76\t    Returns dict with keys:\n77\t    - jailbreak_with_commitment: traces for training/evaluation\n78\t    - jailbreak_no_commitment: excluded jailbreak traces\n79\t    - safe: safe traces for FP evaluation\n80\t    \"\"\"\n81\t    raw_traces = load_harmthoughts(model_name)\n82\t\n83\t    jailbreak_with_commitment = []\n84\t    jailbreak_no_commitment = []\n85\t    safe_traces = []\n86\t\n87\t    for trace_id, steps in raw_traces.items():\n88\t        is_jailbreak = steps[0][\"final_judgment\"] == 1.0\n89\t        labels = [s[\"label\"] for s in steps]\n90\t        sentences = [s[\"sentence\"] for s in steps]\n91\t        query = steps[0][\"query\"]\n92\t        num_steps = len(steps)\n93\t\n94\t        trace_dict = {\n95\t            \"trace_id\": trace_id,\n96\t            \"sentences\": sentences,\n97\t            \"labels\": labels,\n98\t            \"query\": query,\n99\t            \"is_jailbreak\": is_jailbreak,\n100\t            \"num_steps\": num_steps,\n101\t        }\n102\t\n103\t        if not is_jailbreak:\n104\t            trace_dict[\"commitment_point\"] = None\n105\t            trace_dict[\"step_labels\"] = None\n106\t            safe_traces.append(trace_dict)\n107\t            continue\n108\t\n109\t        cp = extract_commitment_point(labels)\n110\t        trace_dict[\"commitment_point\"] = cp\n111\t\n112\t        if cp is not None:\n113\t            trace_dict[\"step_labels\"] = create_step_labels(cp, num_steps)\n114\t            jailbreak_with_commitment.append(trace_dict)\n115\t        else:\n116\t            trace_dict[\"step_labels\"] = None\n117\t            jailbreak_no_commitment.append(trace_dict)\n118\t\n119\t    return {\n120\t        \"jailbreak_with_commitment\": jailbreak_with_commitment,\n121\t        \"jailbreak_no_commitment\": jailbreak_no_commitment,\n122\t        \"safe\": safe_traces,\n123\t    }\n124\t\n125\t\n126\tdef split_data(traces: List[Dict], train_ratio: float = TRAIN_RATIO,\n127\t               val_ratio: float = VAL_RATIO, seed: int = SEED):\n128\t    \"\"\"Split traces into train/val/test at trace level.\"\"\"\n129\t    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n130\t    indices = rng.permutation(len(traces))\n131\t\n132\t    n_train = int(len(traces) * train_ratio)\n133\t    n_val = int(len(traces) * val_ratio)\n134\t\n135\t    train_idx = indices[:n_train]\n136\t    val_idx = indices[n_train:n_train + n_val]\n137\t    test_idx = indices[n_train + n_val:]\n138\t\n139\t    train = [traces[i] for i in train_idx]\n140\t    val = [traces[i] for i in val_idx]\n141\t    test = [traces[i] for i in test_idx]\n142\t\n143\t    return train, val, test\n144\t\n145\t\n146\tdef get_commitment_point_stats(traces: List[Dict]) -> Dict[str, Any]:\n147\t    \"\"\"Compute statistics about commitment point distribution.\"\"\"\n148\t    cps = [t[\"commitment_point\"] for t in traces if t[\"commitment_point\"] is not None]\n149\t    num_steps_list = [t[\"num_steps\"] for t in traces if t[\"commitment_point\"] is not None]\n150\t\n151\t    if not cps:\n152\t        return {\"count\": 0}\n153\t\n154\t    cps = np.array(cps)\n155\t    num_steps = np.array(num_steps_list)\n156\t    relative_positions = cps / num_steps\n157\t\n158\t    return {\n159\t        \"count\": len(cps),\n160\t        \"mean\": float(np.mean(cps)),\n161\t        \"median\": float(np.median(cps)),\n162\t        \"std\": float(np.std(cps)),\n163\t        \"min\": int(np.min(cps)),\n164\t        \"max\": int(np.max(cps)),\n165\t        \"relative_position_mean\": float(np.mean(relative_positions)),\n166\t        \"relative_position_median\": float(np.median(relative_positions)),\n167\t        \"relative_position_std\": float(np.std(relative_positions)),\n168\t    }\n169\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_01GsVfjyjdWjfa357Jwiqk4D","session_id":"e51f3ca8-7ac5-4490-8b2a-be7d501298c2","uuid":"259e1e06-5156-4888-814a-6b70d6e61cf8","timestamp":"2026-05-23T11:32:34.206Z"}

Data Source and Loading (`load_harmthoughts`):**\n- Loads from HuggingFace dataset `ishitakakkar-10/HarmThoughts`\n- Groups by `trace_id` (extracted from `sentence_id` by removing the last step index)\n- Uses `llm_annotation` as the label for each step (line 36)\n\n**2. Commitment Point Extraction (`extract_commitment_point`, lines 48-65):**\n```python\ndef extract_commitment_point(labels: List[str]) -> Optional[int]:\n    \"\"\"Find the commitment point in a sequence of labels.\n    Returns the index after the last safety label, or None if:\n    - No safety labels exist in the trace\n    - The last safety label is at the final position (no post-commitment segment)\n    \"\"\"\n    last_safety_idx = None\n    for i, label in enumerate(labels):\n        if label in SAFETY_LABELS:\n            last_safety_idx = i\n    \n    if last_safety_idx is None:\n        return None\n    if last_safety_idx == len(labels) - 1:\n        return None\n    \n    return last_safety_idx + 1\n```\n\n**3. Step Labels Generation (`create_step_labels`, lines 68-70):**\n```python\ndef create_step_labels(commitment_point: int, num_steps: int) -> List[int]:\n    \"\"\"Generate binary labels: 0 = pre-commitment, 1 = post-commitment.\"\"\"\n    return [0 if i < commitment_point else 1 for i in range(num_steps)]\n```\n\n**4. Safety Labels Definition (config.py, line 45):**\n```python\nSAFETY_LABELS = {\"RA\", \"AL\", \"CC\", \"ED\", \"IA\"}\n```\n\n### Class Filtering:\n\nIn `prepare_dataset()` (lines 73-123), the logic is:\n- **Jailbreak traces only**: Determined by `final_judgment == 1.0` (line 88)\n- **Not class-specific**: The code uses `final_judgment` (a binary indicator) from the first step of each trace, not B-class filtering\n- **Three categories returned**:\n  1. `jailbreak_with_commitment`: Jailbreak traces where commitment point exists\n  2. `jailbreak_no_commitment`: Jailbreak traces without commitment point\n  3. `safe`: All safe traces (where `final_judgment != 1.0`)\n\n### Label Source:\n\n- **step_labels**: Derived from `llm_annotation` (the LLM-annotated safety labels per step)\n- **Not from final_judgment**: The `final_judgment` is only used to classify the entire trace as jailbreak or safe\n\nThe commitment point is the index immediately after the last occurrence of any safety label from {\"RA\", \"AL\", \"CC\", \"ED\", \"IA\"}."}]}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e51f3ca8-7ac5-4490-8b2a-be7d501298c2","uuid":"0d0e57b0-b0ee-4f00-a3c3-55c56ded0e09","timestamp":"2026-05-23T11:32:50.732Z","tool_use_result":{"status":"completed","prompt":"In the project at /home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/contrastive_trace_safety, find how step_labels and commitment_point are generated from the HarmThoughts dataset. Commitment Point Extraction (`extract_commitment_point`, lines 48-65):**\n```python\ndef extract_commitment_point(labels: List[str]) -> Optional[int]:\n    \"\"\"Find the commitment point in a sequence of labels.\n    Returns the index after the last safety label, or None if:\n    - No safety labels exist in the trace\n    - The last safety label is at the final position (no post-commitment segment)\n    \"\"\"\n    last_safety_idx = None\n    for i, label in enumerate(labels):\n        if label in SAFETY_LABELS:\n            last_safety_idx = i\n    \n    if last_safety_idx is None:\n        return None\n    if last_safety_idx == len(labels) - 1:\n        return None\n    \n    return last_safety_idx + 1\n```\n\n**3. The same logistic regression is applied as a surface-level modality baseline.\ndocs/paper/sections/appendix.tex:760:[Omitted long matching line]\ndocs/paper/sections/appendix.tex:764:\\paragraph{Impact on reported results.} All numbers reported in this paper use the corrected text embedding pipeline. The bug affected only QwQ-32B text probe features. The correction, combined with the separate layer selection fix (\\cref{sec:layer-results}), means that early-stage intermediate results (non-significant 32B modality comparisons) reflected two compounding errors: incorrect text features and CR-composite layer selection. Both are fully resolved in the final analysis.\ndocs/paper/sections/appendix.tex:848:To test whether the HS advantage over text probes depends on the specific text encoder, we replace all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (384d) with BGE-large-en-v1.5 (1024d), a 2.7$\\times$ higher-dimensional encoder with stronger semantic representations. \\cref{tab:encoder_robustness} reports precision comparisons at each model's threshold-based layer; \\cref{fig:forest_precision} provides a visual summary.\ndocs/paper/sections/appendix.tex:871:\\caption{Forest plot of HS precision advantage over text probes. Blue: vs.\\ MiniLM-384d; orange: vs.\\ BGE-1024d. Solid markers: survives Holm-Bonferroni; hollow: does not. All 384d comparisons are significant (strong to qualified evidence); against BGE-1024d, only OT-7B (strong) survives correction.}\ndocs/paper/sections/appendix.tex:875:[Omitted long matching line]\ndocs/paper/sections/appendix.tex:904:PCA capacity matching (\\cref{app:encoder_robustness}) preserves 79--92\\% of variance. A natural question is whether the HS advantage depends on PCA's structure-preserving property. We project hidden states to 384d using Gaussian random projection (3 seeds, averaged) and compare with text embeddings at the same dimensionality (\\cref{tab:random_projection}).\ndocs/paper/sections/appendix.tex:909:\\caption{Random projection (RP, 384d) vs.\\ text (384d MiniLM). All $p < 0.001$ (1{,}000 bootstrap resamples). R1-8B uses $W{=}15$ (\\cref{tab:hs_vs_text}: $W{=}3$); QwQ-32B evaluated at L60 (\\cref{tab:hs_vs_text}: L63). 60/20/20 split.}\ndocs/paper/sections/appendix.tex:930:To test whether an LLM-based text encoder closes the HS advantage gap, we encode each reasoning step with E5-Mistral-7B-Instruct~\\citep{wang2024e5mistral} (4096d, reduced to 384d via PCA to match the MiniLM dimensionality). \\cref{tab:e5_mistral} reports precision comparisons against HS probes at threshold-based layers.\ndocs/paper/sections/appendix.tex:935:\\caption{HS vs.\\ E5-Mistral-7B (PCA 384d) precision. HB: Holm-Bonferroni adjusted (4 tests, $\\alpha{=}0.05$). {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01KgfoCrVm4mFz5F3Lfd68AM","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\\section{Experiments}\\label{sec:experiments}\n2\t\n3\tWe evaluate temporal probes across four reasoning models (\\cref{tab:models}), comparing hidden-state (HS) and text modalities under different layer selection criteria.\n4\t\n5\t\\paragraph{Evidence gradient.} We classify HS advantage evidence into three tiers: \\emph{cross-validated}\\footnote{In our usage, \\emph{cross-validated} means the result survives nested 5-fold cross-validation with a held-out test fold, ruling out hyperparameter overfitting. \\emph{Exploratory} ($\\dagger$) results pass HB on the holdout split but lack nested CV validation due to sample constraints.} (nested CV pass; 7--8B models), \\emph{significant} (HB pass without nested CV; QwQ-32B), and \\emph{exploratory} ($^\\dagger$, small sample or narrow coverage; R1-32B). Per-model details follow in \\cref{sec:layer-results,sec:hs-text,sec:32b-analysis}.\n6\t\n7\t\\subsection{Setup}\\label{sec:setup}\n8\t\n9\tWe study R1-8B (Llama-8B, R1-distilled, $n{=}43$ test traces), OT-7B (OpenThinker-7B, trained on the OpenThoughts dataset~\\citep{openthinker}; Qwen-7B backbone, STILL-trained~\\cref{app:ot7b_details}, $n{=}47$), QwQ-32B (Qwen-32B, native reasoning, $n{=}19$--$20$), and R1-32B (Qwen-32B, R1-distilled, $n{=}20$). All traces come from HarmThoughts with step-level CP annotations (60/20/20 split). HS probes extract last-token hidden states from a selected layer; text probes encode each step with all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (384d). Both use L2-regularized logistic regression with temporal windowing $W \\in \\{1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25\\}$ selected on validation. Classification requires $K{=}5$ consecutive threshold crossings (\\cref{tab:k_ablation}). Statistical testing follows \\cref{sec:evaluation}.\\looseness=-1\n10\t\n11\t\\subsection{Layer Selection Results}\\label{sec:layer-results}\n12\t\n13\t\\cref{tab:layer_selection} shows that the crossing-rate composite metric (balanced accuracy $\\times$ crossing rate) systematically selects shallow layers across all four models: L0 for R1-8B (full L0--L24 sweep; \\cref{tab:r18b_full_layer}) and OT-7B, L2 (3\\%) for QwQ-32B, and L10 (16\\%) for R1-32B (\\cref{fig:layer_heatmap}). The three threshold-based metrics (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) share a common confusion matrix and empirically converge to the same deep layer, L63 (98\\%) for both 32B models, consistent with their shared structure, though not mathematically guaranteed. Pure balanced accuracy, a threshold-free metric, selects layers that vary across data splits and models (7--98\\% depth under 60/20/20; \\cref{app:ba_layer_comparison}), itself demonstrating that layer selection is a consequential methodological choice. The mechanism is that shallow layers produce highly compressed representations with small inter-step margins: step-to-step cosine distances are 1.4--2.7$\\times$ smaller than at deep layers across all models (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}). A linear probe in this compressed space operates near the noise floor, generating frequent threshold crossings that inflate crossing rate independently of discriminative power (\\cref{sec:layer-sensitivity}).\\looseness=-1\n14\t\n15\t\\begin{table}[t]\n16\t\\centering\n17\t\\small\n18\t\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n19\t\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n20\t\\toprule\n21\t\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{CR-comp.}$^a$ & \\textbf{Thresh.}$^b$ & $p$ \\textbf{(thresh.)} & $\\Delta$\\textbf{Prec}$^c$ \\\\\n22\t\\midrule\n23\tR1-8B & L0 (0\\%) & L14 (44\\%) & ${<}0.001$ & +20.1 \\\\\n24\tOT-7B & L0 (0\\%) & L16 (57\\%) & ${<}0.001$ & +21.9 \\\\\n25\tQwQ-32B$^\\dagger$ & L2 (3\\%) & L63 (98\\%) & ${<}0.005$ & +19.2 \\\\\n26\tR1-32B$^\\dagger$ & L10 (16\\%) & L63 (98\\%) & ${<}0.001$ & +15.4 \\\\\n27\t\\bottomrule\n28\t\\end{tabular}}\n29\t\\caption{Layer selection sensitivity. $^a$\\,Crossing-rate composite (bal\\_acc $\\times$ crossing\\_rate). $^b$\\,Consensus of threshold-based metrics (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite). $^c$\\,HS$-$text precision difference (pp) at threshold-based layer (\\cref{tab:hs_vs_text}). Rightmost $p$: HS vs.\\ text precision $p$-value at threshold-based layer (e.g., QwQ-32B CR-composite layer: $p{=}0.83$, non-significant). $\\dagger$\\,Sample-limited ($n \\leq 20$).}\n30\t\\label{tab:layer_selection}\n31\t\\end{table}\n32\t\n33\t\\begin{figure}[t]\n34\t\\centering\n35\t\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_layer_heatmap.pdf}\n36\t\\caption{Per-layer metric heatmap (2$\\times$2). Each column: one of five metrics (min-max normalized). Red dashed: CR-composite layer (bal\\_acc $\\times$ crossing\\_rate); green dashed: threshold-based layer (consensus of threshold-based metrics). For R1-8B (full L0--L24 sweep), crossing rate drives CR-composite selection to L0 while threshold-based metrics retain L14. At 32B, CR-composite selections cluster near input layers while threshold-based metrics peak at deep layers.}\n37\t\\label{fig:layer_heatmap}\n38\t\\end{figure}\n39\t\n40\tThe downstream impact is severe: at CR-composite layers, only the two 7--8B models show significant HS advantage; both 32B models fail. The CR-composite's systematic shallow-layer selection is an artifact of cosine compression (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), not a genuine safety signal; random-label controls confirm this distinction (\\cref{app:random_cp}). At threshold-based layers, the HS advantage is graded: cross-validated at 7--8B (OT-7B, R1-8B; nested CV), significant but suggestive of position-competitiveness at QwQ-32B$^\\dagger$, and exploratory at R1-32B$^\\dagger$ (residualized gap below MDE). The $\\dagger$ marks sample-limited test sets ($n \\leq 20$) where effect sizes are descriptive rather than confirmatory. Random-label controls on QwQ-32B and R1-8B confirm that threshold-based deep-layer selection requires genuine safety labels; the geometric shallow-layer bias is Qwen-specific: retained on QwQ-32B but dissolved on R1-8B (\\cref{app:random_cp}). AUROC, excluded from our primary metrics because it is threshold-free (\\cref{sec:evaluation}), confirms HS discriminability at BA-selected layers ($\\Delta$AUROC +12.1 to +15.1pp; \\cref{tab:ba_layer_results}) but cannot diagnose the threshold-dependent layer selection bias.\\looseness=-1\n41\t\n42\t\\paragraph{Ensemble layer selection.} A three-layer ensemble that averages predicted probabilities from the precision-best, BA-best, and inverse-FPR-best layers bypasses single-metric pre-selection entirely. On the holdout split, all four models show significant HS advantage under the ensemble ($p \\leq 0.002$, HB corrected; \\cref{tab:ensemble}), including both 32B models that fail under CR-composite selection. The ensemble succeeds because averaging predictions from layers selected by different threshold-based metrics---which may favor somewhat different depths within the deep region---smooths metric-specific variation and reduces sensitivity to any single metric's layer preference. This holdout result uses the same split for both layer selection and evaluation; cross-validated confirmation is needed before the ensemble can be recommended as a general debiasing strategy (\\cref{sec:discussion}).\\looseness=-1\n43\t\n44\t\\paragraph{Cross-dataset replication (AdvBench).} To test whether metric-induced layer selection bias generalizes beyond HarmThoughts CP labels, we replicate the five-metric layer sweep on AdvBench~\\citep{advbench} (520 traces, 159 jailbreaks) using R1-8B with binary jailbreak/safe labels instead of step-level CP annotations. With held-out evaluation (1{,}000 bootstrap iterations, 60/40 train/test), the CR-composite selects shallow layers (median L2) while precision selects deep layers (median L14), a mean divergence of 31.7pp (95\\% CI [3.2, 48.4]pp, $p{=}0.015$) consistent with HarmThoughts (L0 vs.\\ L14). This confirms that the layer selection bias (C1) is driven by representational geometry, not label semantics; AdvBench lacks step-level CP annotations and thus does not validate the HS advantage (C2) (\\cref{app:advbench_layer_bias}).\\looseness=-1\n45\t\n46\t\\subsection{HS vs.\\ Text Comparison}\\label{sec:hs-text}\n47\t\n48\t\\begin{table}[t]\n49\t\\centering\n50\t\\small\n51\t\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n52\t\\begin{tabular}{llclrrlrc}\n53\t\\toprule\n54\t\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Layer} & \\textbf{Enc.} & $W$ & $\\Delta$\\textbf{BA} & $\\Delta$\\textbf{Prec} & \\textbf{95\\% CI} & $\\Delta$\\textbf{FPR} & \\textbf{HB} \\\\\n55\t\\midrule\n56\tR1-8B & L14 & 384d & 3 & +17.5 & +20.1 & [+12.4, +28.3] & $-$7.9 & \\checkmark \\\\\n57\t & & 1024d & & \\multicolumn{1}{c}{--} & +5.8 & [$-$7.4, +19.5] & \\multicolumn{1}{c}{--} & \\\\[2pt]\n58\tOT-7B & L16 & 384d & 3 & +10.7 & +21.9 & [+14.4, +29.6] & $-$17.1 & \\checkmark \\\\\n59\t & & 1024d & & \\multicolumn{1}{c}{--} & +18.1 & [+8.4, +29.0] & \\multicolumn{1}{c}{--} & \\checkmark \\\\[2pt]\n60\tQwQ$^\\dagger$ & L63 & 384d & 5 & +13.0 & +19.2 & [+9.3, +33.5] & $-$15.0 & \\checkmark \\\\\n61\t & & 1024d & & \\multicolumn{1}{c}{--} & +9.4 & [$-$7.1, +27.7] & \\multicolumn{1}{c}{--} & \\\\[2pt]\n62\tR1-32B$^\\dagger$ & L63 & 384d & 1 & +18.7 & +15.4 & [+8.7, +22.6] & $-$14.6 & \\checkmark \\\\\n63\t & & 1024d & & \\multicolumn{1}{c}{--} & +11.7 & [$-$0.2, +24.3] & \\multicolumn{1}{c}{--} & \\\\\n64\t\\bottomrule\n65\t\\end{tabular}}\n66\t\\caption{HS vs.\\ text at threshold-based layers (LR probes, 60/20/20 split, 10{,}000 bootstrap resamples). Enc.: text encoder (384d = MiniLM-L6-v2, 1024d = BGE-large). $\\Delta$: HS$-$text difference (pp); ``--'' = BA and FPR are not computed for the 1024d comparison because the 384d and 1024d encoders operate at different probe calibration points and do not share a decision threshold. HB: \\checkmark{} = model-level test passes Holm-Bonferroni ($\\alpha{=}0.05$); all 384d model-level tests pass. $^\\dagger$\\,Sample-limited ($n \\leq 20$); 32B results are exploratory. 1024d: HB over 4 model-level precision tests.}\n67\t\\label{tab:hs_vs_text}\n68\t\\end{table}\n69\t\n70\tThe HS advantage is encoder-conditional: against BGE-large (1024d), only OT-7B survives HB correction (\\cref{app:encoder_robustness}). Multi-seed stability appears in \\cref{app:multi_seed}. Against the 384d baseline, HS probes outperform text probes in all four models$^\\dagger$ (\\cref{tab:hs_vs_text}): cross-validated at 7--8B (OT-7B: +21.9pp precision, R1-8B: +20.1pp), significant but suggestive of position-competitiveness at QwQ-32B$^\\dagger$ (+19.2pp), and exploratory at R1-32B$^\\dagger$ (+15.4pp, pattern consistent with position mediation). R1-8B FPR is directionally consistent (5/5 seeds) but not robust ($p{=}0.044$, rank 12/12 in HB step-down; 1/5 seeds Bonferroni; \\cref{app:multi_seed}). R1-32B passes 5/5 seeds but shows a pattern consistent with position mediation (residualized gap NS; \\cref{app:position_residual}); QwQ-32B's position baseline is competitive (\\cref{sec:32b-analysis}). Overall, 18/20 model-seed combinations pass full HB (\\cref{app:multi_seed}). Control task selectivity confirms genuine safety signal: 11.1--16.8pp above chance (\\cref{app:control}). A split-half analysis on OT-7B confirms internal validity (\\cref{app:split_half}).\\looseness=-1\n71\t\n72\t\\paragraph{Capacity control.} PCA reduction of HS features to 384d (79--92\\% variance retained) preserves the precision advantage across all four models$^\\dagger$ (+16.8 to +23.1pp, all $p{<}0.012$, all HB). The control provides partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. Random projection to 384d yields similar results ($\\Delta$Prec +8.3 to +20.5pp; \\cref{app:random_projection}), further weakening the capacity explanation. E5-Mistral-7B (4096d, PCA-reduced to 384d; \\cref{app:e5_mistral}) yields intermediate results (2/4 HB), forming a monotonic encoder-quality gradient with MiniLM (4/4) and BGE-large (1/4).\\looseness=-1\n73\t\n74\t\\paragraph{Encoder robustness.} Against BGE-large~\\citep{xiao2023cpack} (1024d), only OT-7B survives HB (1/4 precision); R1-8B shows adverse FPR direction (+1.2pp). This forms a monotonic encoder-quality gradient: MiniLM-384d (4/4 HB) $\\to$ E5-Mistral-4096d$\\to$384d (2/4) $\\to$ BGE-large-1024d (1/4), with the precision advantage narrowing from +15--22pp to +6--18pp. The HS advantage is thus a function of text encoder capacity (\\cref{fig:forest_precision}); layer selection (Contribution 1) is encoder-independent.\\looseness=-1\n75\t\n76\t\\paragraph{Position mediation.} Position ($t/T$) accounts for 9--20pp of balanced accuracy,\\footnote{R1-32B's 20.4pp drop reflects $W{=}1$; at $W{\\geq}3$, drops are 9--17pp.} but all models retain non-positional signal above chance (all $p{<}0.001$; \\cref{app:position_residual}). Three of four models retain significant non-positional HS advantage (residualized gap +3.7 to +6.9pp, HB corrected); R1-32B's gap is +1.9pp ($p{=}0.914$, $n{=}20$), well below the MDE of ${\\sim}$9pp and thus indistinguishable from zero at current power. Quadratic residualization (adding $(t/T)^2$) is computed for all four models: 2/4 retain significance (R1-8B +10.3pp, OT-7B +9.8pp, both HB), while R1-32B (0.0pp) and QwQ-32B ($-$1.0pp) do not (\\cref{tab:position_quadratic}). QwQ-32B's HS advantage is fully absorbed by the quadratic position term, meaning the information captured by the probe overlaps substantially with non-linear position. Temporal windowing reduces position dependence (\\cref{sec:temporal}).\\looseness=-1\n77\t\n78\t\\paragraph{Caveats.} Nested 5-fold CV confirms the HS precision advantage at 7--8B (10/10 folds, 13--25\\% shrinkage; \\cref{app:nested_cv}); the +21.9pp OT-7B holdout estimate is an upper bound (nested CV selects L21--27 vs.\\ holdout L16). R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ (25\\% coverage); QwQ-32B lead time is negative ($-$1.2 to +1.9 steps). FPR remains 11--50\\%, so current methods characterize the phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors.\\looseness=-1\n79\t\n80\t\\subsection{32B Model-Specific Analysis}\\label{sec:32b-analysis}\n81\t\n82\tResults are exploratory ($n{=}19$--$20$; MDE at 80\\% power: 8.6--9.5pp, \\cref{app:power_analysis}; effects below MDE marked * in \\cref{tab:32b_divergence}). Both 32B models lack nested CV; LOO-CV and multi-seed analyses (\\cref{app:layer_sweep,app:multi_seed}) provide partial robustness checks. R1-32B and QwQ-32B share the Qwen-32B architecture but present opposite detection profiles (\\cref{tab:32b_divergence}); replication with $n{\\geq}50$ traces is needed.\\looseness=-1\n83\t\n84\t\\begin{table}[t]\n85\t\\centering\n86\t\\small\n87\t\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n88\t\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n89\t\\toprule\n90\t\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Cov.} & \\textbf{Lead (steps)} & \\textbf{HB} & \\textbf{Pos.\\ margin} \\\\\n91\t\\midrule\n92\tR1-32B$^\\dagger$ & 25\\% & +11.7 to +13.3 & \\checkmark{} ($W{=}1$) & +14.2pp \\\\\n93\tQwQ-32B$^\\dagger$ & 74\\% & $-$1.2 to +1.9 & \\checkmark{} (all $W$) & $-$3.6pp* \\\\\n94\t\\bottomrule\n95\t\\end{tabular}}\n96\t\\caption{32B cross-model divergence ($\\dagger$\\,$n \\leq 20$, all exploratory; MDE 8.6--9.5pp). HB: passes Holm-Bonferroni ($\\alpha{=}0.05$). Pos.\\ margin: HS BA$-$position baseline. *\\,Below MDE.}\n97\t\\label{tab:32b_divergence}\n98\t\\end{table}\n99\t\n100\tR1-32B suggests commitment 11--13 steps early (+14.2pp over position) but covers only 25\\% of traces ($W{=}1$); its precision effect (+15.4pp) falls below MDE. QwQ-32B achieves significance at all window sizes (74\\% coverage) but with negative lead time; the position baseline achieves higher BA (0.832 vs.\\ 0.796, margin +3.6pp, $p{=}0.133$, NS at $n{=}19$; \\cref{app:position_residual}). Per-model validation is needed.\\looseness=-1\n101\t\n102\t\\subsection{Temporal Context}\\label{sec:temporal}\n103\t\n104\tTemporal windowing ($W{>}1$) improves both modalities (\\cref{fig:window_ablation}): at 7--8B, the modality advantage (+20.1 to +21.9pp) exceeds temporal improvement (+13pp). GRU models achieve comparable accuracy but higher FPR (\\cref{tab:gru_multi_seeds,app:temporal_details}); an adaptive two-stage strategy offers marginal gains (\\cref{app:two_stage}). CP-aligned probe trajectories confirm sharper HS transitions at the commitment point (\\cref{fig:trajectory}); precision gaps across encoder conditions appear in \\cref{fig:forest_precision}.\\looseness=-1\n105\t\n106\t\\subsection{Negative Results}\\label{sec:negative}\n107\t\n108\tNo alternative method consistently matches supervised probing (\\cref{tab:negative}). BOCPD~\\citep{adams2007bayesian}/CUSUM fail (0\\% detection on 3/4 models); Mahalanobis yields near-zero separation; RepE~\\citep{zou2023representation} is model-dependent (BA 0.756 vs.\\ 0.559); MLP probes overfit; CCS misaligns temporally; cross-model transfer fails (\\cref{app:negative_details}). The signal is directional, gradual, model-specific, and linearly separable, which excludes change-point, distance, transfer, and objective-alignment methods respectively.\\looseness=-1\n109\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e51f3ca8-7ac5-4490-8b2a-be7d501298c2","uuid":"65466a78-0168-40e0-ac34-d541ce9200a1","timestamp":"2026-05-23T11:33:33.530Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/docs/paper/sections/experiments.tex","content":"\\section{Experiments}\\label{sec:experiments}\n\nWe evaluate temporal probes across four reasoning models (\\cref{tab:models}), comparing hidden-state (HS) and text modalities under different layer selection criteria.\n\n\\paragraph{Evidence gradient.} We classify HS advantage evidence into three tiers: \\emph{cross-validated}\\footnote{In our usage, \\emph{cross-validated} means the result survives nested 5-fold cross-validation with a held-out test fold, ruling out hyperparameter overfitting. \\emph{Exploratory} ($\\dagger$) results pass HB on the holdout split but lack nested CV validation due to sample constraints.} (nested CV pass; 7--8B models), \\emph{significant} (HB pass without nested CV; QwQ-32B), and \\emph{exploratory} ($^\\dagger$, small sample or narrow coverage; R1-32B). Per-model details follow in \\cref{sec:layer-results,sec:hs-text,sec:32b-analysis}.\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\\label{sec:setup}\n\nWe study R1-8B (Llama-8B, R1-distilled, $n{=}43$ test traces), OT-7B (OpenThinker-7B, trained on the OpenThoughts dataset~\\citep{openthinker}; Qwen-7B backbone, STILL-trained~\\cref{app:ot7b_details}, $n{=}47$), QwQ-32B (Qwen-32B, native reasoning, $n{=}19$--$20$), and R1-32B (Qwen-32B, R1-distilled, $n{=}20$). All traces come from HarmThoughts with step-level CP annotations (60/20/20 split). HS probes extract last-token hidden states from a selected layer; text probes encode each step with all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (384d). Both use L2-regularized logistic regression with temporal windowing $W \\in \\{1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25\\}$ selected on validation. Classification requires $K{=}5$ consecutive threshold crossings (\\cref{tab:k_ablation}). Statistical testing follows \\cref{sec:evaluation}.\\looseness=-1\n\n\\subsection{Layer Selection Results}\\label{sec:layer-results}\n\n\\cref{tab:layer_selection} shows that the crossing-rate composite metric (balanced accuracy $\\times$ crossing rate) systematically selects shallow layers across all four models: L0 for R1-8B (full L0--L24 sweep; \\cref{tab:r18b_full_layer}) and OT-7B, L2 (3\\%) for QwQ-32B, and L10 (16\\%) for R1-32B (\\cref{fig:layer_heatmap}). The three threshold-based metrics (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) share a common confusion matrix and empirically converge to the same deep layer, L63 (98\\%) for both 32B models, consistent with their shared structure, though not mathematically guaranteed. Pure balanced accuracy, a threshold-free metric, selects layers that vary across data splits and models (7--98\\% depth under 60/20/20; \\cref{app:ba_layer_comparison}), itself demonstrating that layer selection is a consequential methodological choice. The mechanism is that shallow layers produce highly compressed representations with small inter-step margins: step-to-step cosine distances are 1.4--2.7$\\times$ smaller than at deep layers across all models (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}). A linear probe in this compressed space operates near the noise floor, generating frequent threshold crossings that inflate crossing rate independently of discriminative power (\\cref{sec:layer-sensitivity}).\\looseness=-1\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{CR-comp.}$^a$ & \\textbf{Thresh.}$^b$ & $p$ \\textbf{(thresh.)} & $\\Delta$\\textbf{Prec}$^c$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nR1-8B & L0 (0\\%) & L14 (44\\%) & ${<}0.001$ & +20.1 \\\\\nOT-7B & L0 (0\\%) & L16 (57\\%) & ${<}0.001$ & +21.9 \\\\\nQwQ-32B$^\\dagger$ & L2 (3\\%) & L63 (98\\%) & ${<}0.005$ & +19.2 \\\\\nR1-32B$^\\dagger$ & L10 (16\\%) & L63 (98\\%) & ${<}0.001$ & +15.4 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Layer selection sensitivity. $^a$\\,Crossing-rate composite (bal\\_acc $\\times$ crossing\\_rate). $^b$\\,Consensus of threshold-based metrics (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite). $^c$\\,HS$-$text precision difference (pp) at threshold-based layer (\\cref{tab:hs_vs_text}). Rightmost $p$: HS vs.\\ text precision $p$-value at threshold-based layer (e.g., QwQ-32B CR-composite layer: $p{=}0.83$, non-significant). $\\dagger$\\,Sample-limited ($n \\leq 20$).}\n\\label{tab:layer_selection}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_layer_heatmap.pdf}\n\\caption{Per-layer metric heatmap (2$\\times$2). Each column: one of five metrics (min-max normalized). Red dashed: CR-composite layer (bal\\_acc $\\times$ crossing\\_rate); green dashed: threshold-based layer (consensus of threshold-based metrics). For R1-8B (full L0--L24 sweep), crossing rate drives CR-composite selection to L0 while threshold-based metrics retain L14. At 32B, CR-composite selections cluster near input layers while threshold-based metrics peak at deep layers.}\n\\label{fig:layer_heatmap}\n\\end{figure}\n\nThe downstream impact is severe: at CR-composite layers, only the two 7--8B models show significant HS advantage; both 32B models fail. The CR-composite's systematic shallow-layer selection is an artifact of cosine compression (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), not a genuine safety signal; random-label controls confirm this distinction (\\cref{app:random_cp}). At threshold-based layers, the HS advantage is graded: cross-validated at 7--8B (OT-7B, R1-8B; nested CV), significant but suggestive of position-competitiveness at QwQ-32B$^\\dagger$, and exploratory at R1-32B$^\\dagger$ (residualized gap below MDE). The $\\dagger$ marks sample-limited test sets ($n \\leq 20$) where effect sizes are descriptive rather than confirmatory. Random-label controls on QwQ-32B and R1-8B confirm that threshold-based deep-layer selection requires genuine safety labels; the geometric shallow-layer bias is Qwen-specific: retained on QwQ-32B but dissolved on R1-8B (\\cref{app:random_cp}). AUROC, excluded from our primary metrics because it is threshold-free (\\cref{sec:evaluation}), confirms HS discriminability at BA-selected layers ($\\Delta$AUROC +12.1 to +15.1pp; \\cref{tab:ba_layer_results}) but cannot diagnose the threshold-dependent layer selection bias.\\looseness=-1\n\n\\paragraph{Ensemble layer selection.} A three-layer ensemble that averages predicted probabilities from the precision-best, BA-best, and inverse-FPR-best layers bypasses single-metric pre-selection entirely. On the holdout split, all four models show significant HS advantage under the ensemble ($p \\leq 0.002$, HB corrected; \\cref{tab:ensemble}), including both 32B models that fail under CR-composite selection. The ensemble succeeds because averaging predictions from layers selected by different threshold-based metrics---which may favor somewhat different depths within the deep region---smooths metric-specific variation and reduces sensitivity to any single metric's layer preference. This holdout result uses the same split for both layer selection and evaluation; cross-validated confirmation is needed before the ensemble can be recommended as a general debiasing strategy (\\cref{sec:discussion}).\\looseness=-1\n\n\\paragraph{Cross-dataset replication (AdvBench).} To test whether metric-induced layer selection bias generalizes beyond HarmThoughts CP labels, we replicate the five-metric layer sweep on AdvBench~\\citep{advbench} (520 traces, 159 jailbreaks) using R1-8B with binary jailbreak/safe labels instead of step-level CP annotations. With held-out evaluation (1{,}000 bootstrap iterations, 60/40 train/test), the CR-composite selects shallow layers (median L2) while precision selects deep layers (median L14), a mean divergence of 31.7pp (95\\% CI [3.2, 48.4]pp, $p{=}0.015$) consistent with HarmThoughts (L0 vs.\\ L14). This confirms that the layer selection bias (C1) is driven by representational geometry, not label semantics; AdvBench lacks step-level CP annotations and thus does not validate the HS advantage (C2) (\\cref{app:advbench_layer_bias}).\\looseness=-1\n\n\\subsection{HS vs.\\ Text Comparison}\\label{sec:hs-text}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{llclrrlrc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Layer} & \\textbf{Enc.} & $W$ & $\\Delta$\\textbf{BA} & $\\Delta$\\textbf{Prec} & \\textbf{95\\% CI} & $\\Delta$\\textbf{FPR} & \\textbf{HB} \\\\\n\\midrule\nR1-8B & L14 & 384d & 3 & +17.5 & +20.1 & [+12.4, +28.3] & $-$7.9 & \\checkmark \\\\\n & & 1024d & & \\multicolumn{1}{c}{--} & +5.8 & [$-$7.4, +19.5] & \\multicolumn{1}{c}{--} & \\\\[2pt]\nOT-7B & L16 & 384d & 3 & +10.7 & +21.9 & [+14.4, +29.6] & $-$17.1 & \\checkmark \\\\\n & & 1024d & & \\multicolumn{1}{c}{--} & +18.1 & [+8.4, +29.0] & \\multicolumn{1}{c}{--} & \\checkmark \\\\[2pt]\nQwQ$^\\dagger$ & L63 & 384d & 5 & +13.0 & +19.2 & [+9.3, +33.5] & $-$15.0 & \\checkmark \\\\\n & & 1024d & & \\multicolumn{1}{c}{--} & +9.4 & [$-$7.1, +27.7] & \\multicolumn{1}{c}{--} & \\\\[2pt]\nR1-32B$^\\dagger$ & L63 & 384d & 1 & +18.7 & +15.4 & [+8.7, +22.6] & $-$14.6 & \\checkmark \\\\\n & & 1024d & & \\multicolumn{1}{c}{--} & +11.7 & [$-$0.2, +24.3] & \\multicolumn{1}{c}{--} & \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{HS vs.\\ text at threshold-based layers (LR probes, 60/20/20 split, 10{,}000 bootstrap resamples). Enc.: text encoder (384d = MiniLM-L6-v2, 1024d = BGE-large). $\\Delta$: HS$-$text difference (pp); ``--'' = BA and FPR are not computed for the 1024d comparison because the 384d and 1024d encoders operate at different probe calibration points and do not share a decision threshold. HB: \\checkmark{} = model-level test passes Holm-Bonferroni ($\\alpha{=}0.05$); all 384d model-level tests pass. $^\\dagger$\\,Sample-limited ($n \\leq 20$); 32B results are exploratory. 1024d: HB over 4 model-level precision tests.}\n\\label{tab:hs_vs_text}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe HS advantage is encoder-conditional: against BGE-large (1024d), only OT-7B survives HB correction (\\cref{app:encoder_robustness}). Multi-seed stability appears in \\cref{app:multi_seed}. Against the 384d baseline, HS probes outperform text probes in all four models$^\\dagger$ (\\cref{tab:hs_vs_text}): cross-validated at 7--8B (OT-7B: +21.9pp precision, R1-8B: +20.1pp), significant but suggestive of position-competitiveness at QwQ-32B$^\\dagger$ (+19.2pp), and exploratory at R1-32B$^\\dagger$ (+15.4pp, pattern consistent with position mediation). R1-8B FPR is directionally consistent (5/5 seeds) but not robust ($p{=}0.044$, rank 12/12 in HB step-down; 1/5 seeds Bonferroni; \\cref{app:multi_seed}). R1-32B passes 5/5 seeds but shows a pattern consistent with position mediation (residualized gap NS; \\cref{app:position_residual}); QwQ-32B's position baseline is competitive (\\cref{sec:32b-analysis}). Overall, 18/20 model-seed combinations pass full HB (\\cref{app:multi_seed}). Control task selectivity confirms genuine safety signal: 11.1--16.8pp above chance (\\cref{app:control}). A split-half analysis on OT-7B confirms internal validity (\\cref{app:split_half}).\\looseness=-1\n\n\\paragraph{Capacity control.} PCA reduction of HS features to 384d (79--92\\% variance retained) preserves the precision advantage across all four models$^\\dagger$ (+16.8 to +23.1pp, all $p{<}0.012$, all HB). The control provides partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. Random projection to 384d yields similar results ($\\Delta$Prec +8.3 to +20.5pp; \\cref{app:random_projection}), further weakening the capacity explanation. E5-Mistral-7B (4096d, PCA-reduced to 384d; \\cref{app:e5_mistral}) yields intermediate results (2/4 HB), forming a monotonic encoder-quality gradient with MiniLM (4/4) and BGE-large (1/4).\\looseness=-1\n\n\\paragraph{Encoder robustness.} Against BGE-large~\\citep{xiao2023cpack} (1024d), only OT-7B survives HB (1/4 precision); R1-8B shows adverse FPR direction (+1.2pp). This forms a monotonic encoder-quality gradient: MiniLM-384d (4/4 HB) $\\to$ E5-Mistral-4096d$\\to$384d (2/4) $\\to$ BGE-large-1024d (1/4), with the precision advantage narrowing from +15--22pp to +6--18pp. The HS advantage is thus a function of text encoder capacity (\\cref{fig:forest_precision}); layer selection (Contribution 1) is encoder-independent.\\looseness=-1\n\n\\paragraph{Position mediation.} Position ($t/T$) accounts for 9--20pp of balanced accuracy,\\footnote{R1-32B's 20.4pp drop reflects $W{=}1$; at $W{\\geq}3$, drops are 9--17pp.} but all models retain non-positional signal above chance (all $p{<}0.001$; \\cref{app:position_residual}). Three of four models retain significant non-positional HS advantage (residualized gap +3.7 to +6.9pp, HB corrected); R1-32B's gap is +1.9pp ($p{=}0.914$, $n{=}20$), well below the MDE of ${\\sim}$9pp and thus indistinguishable from zero at current power. Quadratic residualization (adding $(t/T)^2$) is computed for all four models: 2/4 retain significance (R1-8B +10.3pp, OT-7B +9.8pp, both HB), while R1-32B (0.0pp) and QwQ-32B ($-$1.0pp) do not (\\cref{tab:position_quadratic}). QwQ-32B's HS advantage is fully absorbed by the quadratic position term, meaning the information captured by the probe overlaps substantially with non-linear position. Temporal windowing reduces position dependence (\\cref{sec:temporal}).\\looseness=-1\n\n\\paragraph{Caveats.} Nested 5-fold CV confirms the HS precision advantage at 7--8B (10/10 folds, 13--25\\% shrinkage; \\cref{app:nested_cv}); the +21.9pp OT-7B holdout estimate is an upper bound (nested CV selects L21--27 vs.\\ holdout L16). R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ (25\\% coverage); QwQ-32B lead time is negative ($-$1.2 to +1.9 steps). FPR remains 11--50\\%, so current methods characterize the phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors.\\looseness=-1\n\n\\subsection{32B Model-Specific Analysis}\\label{sec:32b-analysis}\n\nResults are exploratory ($n{=}19$--$20$; MDE at 80\\% power: 8.6--9.5pp, \\cref{app:power_analysis}; effects below MDE marked * in \\cref{tab:32b_divergence}). Both 32B models lack nested CV; LOO-CV and multi-seed analyses (\\cref{app:layer_sweep,app:multi_seed}) provide partial robustness checks. R1-32B and QwQ-32B share the Qwen-32B architecture but present opposite detection profiles (\\cref{tab:32b_divergence}); replication with $n{\\geq}50$ traces is needed.\\looseness=-1\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Cov.} & \\textbf{Lead (steps)} & \\textbf{HB} & \\textbf{Pos.\\ margin} \\\\\n\\midrule\nR1-32B$^\\dagger$ & 25\\% & +11.7 to +13.3 & \\checkmark{} ($W{=}1$) & +14.2pp \\\\\nQwQ-32B$^\\dagger$ & 74\\% & $-$1.2 to +1.9 & \\checkmark{} (all $W$) & $-$3.6pp* \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{32B cross-model divergence ($\\dagger$\\,$n \\leq 20$, all exploratory; MDE 8.6--9.5pp). HB: passes Holm-Bonferroni ($\\alpha{=}0.05$). Pos.\\ margin: HS BA$-$position baseline. *\\,Below MDE.}\n\\label{tab:32b_divergence}\n\\end{table}\n\nR1-32B suggests commitment 11--13 steps early (+14.2pp over position) but covers only 25\\% of traces ($W{=}1$); its precision effect (+15.4pp) falls below MDE. QwQ-32B achieves significance at all window sizes (74\\% coverage) but with negative lead time; the position baseline achieves higher BA (0.832 vs.\\ 0.796, margin +3.6pp, $p{=}0.133$, NS at $n{=}19$; \\cref{app:position_residual}). Per-model validation is needed.\\looseness=-1\n\n\\subsection{Temporal Context}\\label{sec:temporal}\n\nTemporal windowing ($W{>}1$) improves both modalities (\\cref{fig:window_ablation}): at 7--8B, the modality advantage (+20.1 to +21.9pp) exceeds temporal improvement (+13pp). GRU models achieve comparable accuracy but higher FPR (\\cref{tab:gru_multi_seeds,app:temporal_details}); an adaptive two-stage strategy offers marginal gains (\\cref{app:two_stage}). CP-aligned probe trajectories confirm sharper HS transitions at the commitment point (\\cref{fig:trajectory}); precision gaps across encoder conditions appear in \\cref{fig:forest_precision}.\\looseness=-1\n\n\\subsection{Negative Results}\\label{sec:negative}\n\nNo alternative method consistently matches supervised probing (\\cref{tab:negative}). BOCPD~\\citep{adams2007bayesian}/CUSUM fail (0\\% detection on 3/4 models); Mahalanobis yields near-zero separation; RepE~\\citep{zou2023representation} is model-dependent (BA 0.756 vs.\\ 0.559); MLP probes overfit; CCS misaligns temporally; cross-model transfer fails (\\cref{app:negative_details}). The signal is directional, gradual, model-specific, and linearly separable, which excludes change-point, distance, transfer, and objective-alignment methods respectively.\\looseness=-1\n","numLines":109,"startLine":1,"totalLines":109}}}
